Let me understand the full scope first, then write a script to generate the proof. 

Ran terminal command:  cd /home/john/Reps/Sophie && cat > _gen_mordell.py << 'PYEOF'
# Generate explicit witnesses for each n%840 residue class
# For each r in 0..839 not in exceptions, find x,y,z as functions of q where n = 840*q + r
# We'll use the parametric families to derive witnesses

EXCEPTIONS = {1, 121, 169, 289, 361, 529}

def check_witness(n, x, y, z):
    """Verify 4/n = 1/x + 1/y + 1/z in rationals"""
    from fractions import Fraction
    if x <= 0 or y <= 0 or z <= 0:
        return False
    return Fraction(4, n) == Fraction(1, x) + Fraction(1, y) + Fraction(1, z)

def find_witness(n):
    """Find a witness triple for 4/n = 1/x + 1/y + 1/z"""
    from fractions import Fraction
    F = Fraction(4, n)
    # Try various parametric families
    # Family: 4/n = 1/a + 1/b + 1/c
    # Simple: if n even, n=2m: 1/m + 1/(2m) + 1/(2m)
    if n % 2 == 0:
        m = n // 2
        return (m, 2*m, 2*m)
    # n odd: if n%4==3, n=4k+3, k=(n-3)//4: 1/(k+2) + 1/((k+1)(k+2)) + 1/(n(k+1))
    if n % 4 == 3:
        k = (n - 3) // 4
        return (k+2, (k+1)*(k+2), n*(k+1))
    # n%8==5: n=8k+5, k=(n-5)//8: 1/(2(k+1)) + 1/(n(k+1)) + 1/(2n(k+1))
    if n % 8 == 5:
        k = (n - 5) // 8
        return (2*(k+1), n*(k+1), 2*n*(k+1))
    # n%3==2: n=3m+2, m=(n-2)//3: 1/(m+1) + 1/n + 1/(n(m+1))
    if n % 3 == 2:
        m = (n - 2) // 3
        return (m+1, n, n*(m+1))
    # n%3==0: n=3m: 1/m + 1/(4m) + 1/(12m)
    if n % 3 == 0:
        m = n // 3
        return (m, 4*m, 12*m)
    # n%7==6: n=7j+6, j=(n-6)//7: 1/(2(j+1)) + 1/(2n) + 1/(2n(j+1))
    if n % 7 == 6:
        j = (n - 6) // 7
        return (2*(j+1), 2*n, 2*n*(j+1))
    # n%7==5: n=7j+5, j=(n-5)//7: 1/(2(j+1)) + 1/(2n) + 1/(n(j+1))
    if n % 7 == 5:
        j = (n - 5) // 7
        return (2*(j+1), 2*n, n*(j+1))
    # n%7==3: n=7j+3, j=(n-3)//7: 1/(2j+1) + 1/(2n) + 1/(2n(2j+1))
    if n % 7 == 3:
        j = (n - 3) // 7
        return (2*j+1, 2*n, 2*n*(2*j+1))
    # n%7==0: n=7m: try 4/(7m) = ?
    if n % 7 == 0:
        m = n // 7
        # 4/(7m) = 1/(2m) + 1/(7m) + 1/(14m) ? check: 7+2+1=10 ≠ 8 no
        # Try n%5 families
        pass
    # n%5==0: n=5m: 4/(5m) = 1/(2m) + 1/(5m) + 1/(10m)? check: 5+2+1=8/10 no
    # 1/(2m)+1/(5m)+1/(10m) = 5/(10m)+2/(10m)+1/(10m) = 8/(10m) = 4/(5m) ✓
    if n % 5 == 0:
        m = n // 5
        return (2*m, 5*m, 10*m)
    # n%5==3: n=5m+3: check if any family works
    if n % 5 == 3:
        m = (n - 3) // 5
        # 4/(5m+3): try 1/(2m+1) + ...
        # Family B: n*a+b+c=4abcd. Try a=1,b=1,c=2m+1,d=1: n+1+(2m+1)=8m+5? Not 4*(2m+1)
        # Try a=1,b=1,c=?,d=?: need n+b+c=4bc... 
        # (5m+3) + b + c = 4bc
        # Brute force small values
        for b in range(1, 20):
            for c in range(b, 100*n):
                if n + b + c == 4*b*c:
                    return (b*c, n*b, n*c)
    # n%5==2: n=5m+2:
    if n % 5 == 2:
        m = (n - 2) // 5
        # Similar search
        for b in range(1, 20):
            for c in range(b, 100*n):
                if n + b + c == 4*b*c:
                    return (b*c, n*b, n*c)
    # Generic brute force
    for x in range(1, 4*n+1):
        rem = Fraction(4, n) - Fraction(1, x)
        if rem <= 0:
            break
        for y in range(x, 4*n+1):
            rem2 = rem - Fraction(1, y)
            if rem2 <= 0 or rem2.denominator > 10**8:
                break
            if rem2.numerator == 1:
                z = rem2.denominator
                if z >= y:
                    return (x, y, z)
    return None

# Test all residues mod 840
failures = []
witnesses = {}
for r in range(840):
    if r in EXCEPTIONS:
        continue
    # Test with a few specific n values
    test_ns = [840 + r, 840*2 + r, 840*3 + r]
    if r < 2:
        test_ns = [840 + r if r + 840 >= 2 else 840*2 + r]
    for n in test_ns:
        if n < 2:
            continue
        w = find_witness(n)
        if w is None:
            failures.append((r, n))
        elif not check_witness(n, *w):
            failures.append((r, n, w, "wrong"))
        break

print(f"Total failures: {len(failures)}")

from fractions import Fraction

EXCEPTIONS = {1, 121, 169, 289, 361, 529}

def check_witness(n, x, y, z):
    if x <= 0 or y <= 0 or z <= 0: return False
    return Fraction(4, n) == Fraction(1, x) + Fraction(1, y) + Fraction(1, z)

def brute_force(n):
    F = Fraction(4, n)
    for x in range(1, 5*n):
        r = F - Fraction(1, x)
        if r <= 0: break
        for y in range(x, 5*n):
            r2 = r - Fraction(1, y)
            if r2 <= 0: break
            if r2.numerator == 1 and r2.denominator >= y:
                return (x, y, r2.denominator)
    return None

# Check the 8 failures
for r, n in [(49, 889), (193, 1033), (217, 1057), (337, 1177), (457, 1297), (553, 1393), (673, 1513), (721, 1561)]:
    print(f'r={r} (mod840={r%840}): n={n}')
    print(f'  n%2={n%2}, n%4={n%4}, n%8={n%8}, n%3={n%3}, n%5={n%5}, n%7={n%7}, n%11={n%11}')
    w = brute_force(n)
    print(f'  brute witness: {w}')
    if w: print(f'  verify: {check_witness(n,*w)}')
    print()

from fractions import Fraction

def brute_force_wide(n):
    F = Fraction(4, n)
    for x in range(1, 100*n):
        r = F - Fraction(1, x)
        if r <= 0: break
        for y in range(x, 100*n):
            r2 = r - Fraction(1, y)
            if r2 <= 0: break
            if r2.numerator == 1 and r2.denominator >= y:

    return None

from fractions import Fraction
import math

def find_4n(n):
    'Find 4/n = 1/x + 1/y + 1/z using smart search'
    # x ranges from ceil(n/4) to n (since 1/x <= 4/n means x >= n/4)
    for x in range(math.ceil(n/4), 5*n+1):
        num = 4*x - n  # numerator of remainder (4/n - 1/x) = (4x-n)/(nx)
        if num <= 0:
            continue
        denom = n * x
        # Now solve num/denom = 1/y + 1/z where y <= z
        # y ranges from ceil(denom/num) to 2*denom//num
        y_min = math.ceil(denom / num)
        y_max = 2 * denom // num
        for y in range(y_min, y_max + 1):
            # z = y*denom / (y*num - denom)
            yd = y * num - denom
            if yd <= 0:
                continue
            if (y * denom) % yd == 0:
                z = y * denom // yd
                if z >= y:
                    # verify
                    if Fraction(4, n) == Fraction(1, x) + Fraction(1, y) + Fraction(1, z):
                        return (x, y, z)
    return None

for r in [49, 193, 217, 337, 457, 553, 673, 721]:
    n = 840 + r
    w = find_4n(n)
    print(f'r={r}, n={n}: {w}')
    if w:
        x, y, z = w
        print(f'  in terms of k=n//840={n//840}: x={x}, y={y}, z={z}')

from fractions import Fraction
import math

def find_4n(n):
    for x in range(math.ceil(n/4), 5*n+1):
        num = 4*x - n
        if num <= 0: continue
        denom = n * x
        y_min = math.ceil(denom / num)
        y_max = 2 * denom // num
        for y in range(y_min, y_max + 1):
            yd = y * num - denom
            if yd <= 0: continue
            if (y * denom) % yd == 0:
                z = y * denom // yd
                if z >= y and Fraction(4,n)==Fraction(1,x)+Fraction(1,y)+Fraction(1,z):
                    return (x, y, z)
    return None

for r in [49, 193, 217, 337, 457, 553, 673, 721]:

from fractions import Fraction
import math

EXCEPTIONS = {1, 121, 169, 289, 361, 529}

def check(n, x, y, z):
    return x>0 and y>0 and z>0 and Fraction(4,n)==Fraction(1,x)+Fraction(1,y)+Fraction(1,z)

def parametric_witness(n):
    """Try to find a witness WITH a parametric formula that generalizes."""
    # All these give witnesses linear in n
    if n % 2 == 0:
        m = n // 2
        return (m, 2*m, 2*m, "even")
    if n % 4 == 3:
        k = (n-3)//4
        return (k+2, (k+1)*(k+2), n*(k+1), "3mod4")
    if n % 8 == 5:
        k = (n-5)//8
        return (2*(k+1), n*(k+1), 2*n*(k+1), "5mod8")
    if n % 3 == 0:
        m = n//3
        return (m, 4*m, 12*m, "0mod3")
    if n % 3 == 2:
        m = (n-2)//3
        return (m+1, n, n*(m+1), "2mod3")    
    if n % 7 == 6:
        j = (n-6)//7
        return (2*(j+1), 2*n, 2*n*(j+1), "6mod7")
    if n % 7 == 5:
        j = (n-5)//7
        return (2*(j+1), 2*n, n*(j+1), "5mod7")
    if n % 7 == 3:
        j = (n-3)//7
        return (2*j+1, 2*n, 2*n*(2*j+1), "3mod7")
    if n % 7 == 0:
        m = n//7
        return (3*m, 6*m, 14*m, "0mod7")  # 1/(3m)+1/(6m)+1/(14m) = 4/(7m)
    if n % 5 == 0:
        m = n//5
        return (2*m, 5*m, 10*m, "0mod5")
    return None

# Verify all simple cases
failures = []
for r in range(840):
    if r in EXCEPTIONS: continue
    n = 840*10+r
    if n < 2: n = 840+r
    w = parametric_witness(n)
    if w is None:
        failures.append(r)
    elif not check(n, w[0], w[1], w[2]):
        failures.append((r, n, w, "WRONG"))

print(f"After adding 0mod7 and 0mod5 families: {len(failures)} failures")
print(f"Remaining: {failures}")

def find_4n_smart(n):
    """Find 4/n = 1/x + 1/y + 1/z, starting from x=ceil(n/4)"""
    for x in range(math.ceil(n/4), 8*n+1):
        num = 4*x - n
        if num <= 0: continue
        denom = n * x
        y_min = math.ceil(denom / num)
        y_max = 2 * denom // num
        for y in range(y_min, y_max + 1):
            yd = y * num - denom
            if yd <= 0: continue
            if (y * denom) % yd == 0:
                z = y * denom // yd
                if z >= y and Fraction(4,n)==Fraction(1,x)+Fraction(1,y)+Fraction(1,z):
                    return (x, y, z)
    return None

from fractions import Fraction
import math

def find_4n_smart(n):
    for x in range(math.ceil(n/4), 8*n+1):
        num = 4*x - n
        if num <= 0: continue
        denom = n * x
        y_min = math.ceil(denom / num)
        y_max = 2 * denom // num
        for y in range(y_min, y_max + 1):
            yd = y * num - denom
            if yd <= 0: continue
            if (y * denom) % yd == 0:
                z = y * denom // yd
                if z >= y and Fraction(4,n)==Fraction(1,x)+Fraction(1,y)+Fraction(1,z):
                    return (x, y, z)
    return None

# Check if x = (n + delta) / 4 for some small delta
for r in [193, 337, 457, 673, 697, 793]:
    n0 = r  # k=0
    if n0 < 2: n0 = 840 + r
    w = find_4n_smart(n0)
    if w:
        x0, y0, z0 = w
        # x = (n + delta)/4 => delta = 4*x - n
        delta_x = 4*x0 - n0
        # y = n*A + B => A = y//n, B = y%n
        A_y = y0 // n0
        B_y = y0 % n0
        print(f"r={r}: n={n0}, x={x0}, y={y0}, z={z0}")
        print(f"  4x-n={delta_x}, y=n*{A_y}+{B_y}")
        # Verify the formula: x=(n+{delta_x})/4, y=n*{A_y}+{B_y}
        # Try to guess z as a polynomial in n
        for k in [1, 2, 3]:
            n_k = 840*k + r
            w_k = find_4n_smart(n_k)
            if w_k:
                x_k, y_k, z_k = w_k
                dx_k = 4*x_k - n_k
                if dx_k == delta_x:
                    print(f"  k={k}: n={n_k}, 4x-n={dx_k} ✓, y=n*{y_k//n_k}+{y_k%n_k}, z={z_k}")
                else:
                    print(f"  k={k}: 4x-n={dx_k} (differs from {delta_x})")
    print()

from fractions import Fraction
import math

EXCEPTIONS = {1, 121, 169, 289, 361, 529}

def find_4n_smart(n):
    for x in range(math.ceil(n/4), 8*n+1):
        num = 4*x - n
        if num <= 0: continue
        denom = n * x
        y_min = math.ceil(denom / num)
        y_max = 2 * denom // num
        for y in range(y_min, y_max + 1):

            if yd <= 0: continue
            if (y * denom) % yd == 0:
                z = y * denom // yd
                if z >= y and Fraction(4,n)==Fraction(1,x)+Fraction(1,y)+Fraction(1,z):

    return None

def check(n, x, y, z):
    return x>0 and y>0 and z>0 and Fraction(4,n)==Fraction(1,x)+Fraction(1,y)+Fraction(1,z)

def witness_lean(n):
    """Generate the Lean witness for n in terms of k = n//840"""
    # First try parametric families
    if n % 2 == 0:
        m = n // 2
        return f"(n/2, n, n)", None  # placeholder, will use erdos_straus_even
    if n % 4 == 3:
        return None, "three_mod4"
    if n % 8 == 5:
        return None, "five_mod8"
    if n % 3 == 0:
        m = n // 3
        return (m, 4*m, 12*m), "zero_mod3"
    if n % 3 == 2:
        return None, "two_mod3"
    if n % 7 == 6:
        return None, "six_mod7"
    if n % 7 == 5:
        return None, "five_mod7"
    if n % 7 == 3:
        return None, "three_mod7"
    if n % 7 == 0:
        m = n // 7
        return (3*m, 6*m, 14*m), "zero_mod7"
    if n % 5 == 0:
        m = n // 5
        return (2*m, 5*m, 10*m), "zero_mod5"
    # Need explicit witness
    w = find_4n_smart(n)
    return w, "explicit"

from fractions import Fraction
import math

# For each hard residue, we have x = (n + delta)/4 where delta is constant.
# The witness is: 4/n = 1/x + (something)
# Let me derive the exact parametric formula symbolically.

# For r=337, delta=3: x = (n+3)/4
# 4/n - 1/x = 4/n - 4/(n+3) = (4(n+3) - 4n)/(n(n+3)) = 12/(n(n+3))
# So we need 12/(n(n+3)) = 1/y + 1/z
# This means 4/(n(n+3)/3) expressed as two unit fractions.
# Note: gcd(n, n+3) | 3. For n%3=1, gcd(n,n+3)=gcd(n,3)=1
# So 12/(n(n+3)) = 1/y + 1/z with y,z positive integers.
# Try y = n(n+3)/6: then 12/(n(n+3)) - 6/(n(n+3)) = 6/(n(n+3)) = 1/z => z=n(n+3)/6
# That would need 6|n(n+3). n≡1 mod 8, n≡1 mod 3, n≡2 mod 7... 
# n(n+3) where n≡1mod8, n≡1mod3: n(n+3) ≡ 1*4=4 mod 8 (since n+3≡4mod8), so NOT divisible by 8
# But we need 6|n(n+3). n≡1mod3 => n+3≡1mod3 => n(n+3)≡1mod3. So 3∤n(n+3)!
# So y=z=n(n+3)/6 won't work for r=337.

# KEY DISCOVERY (from previous output):
# For r=337, 457, 697, 193: y = P//c + 2 where P=n*x, c=3 or 7, consistently!
# For r=673, 793: alternates between c=7 and c=3

# For r=337, c=3: P = n*(n+3)/4. P ≡ ? mod 3
# n≡337%3=1 mod 3, n+3≡1 mod 3, n(n+3)≡1 mod 3. P=n(n+3)/4. 
# n(n+3)≡1 mod 3 => P = n(n+3)/4 ≡ 1*inv(4) mod 3 = 1*1=1 mod 3 (inv(4)≡1 mod 3)
# So P≡1 mod 3, P//3 gives remainder 1, P = 3q+1, y = q+2 = (P+5)/3... not a polynomial

# But SYMBOLIC formula: y = (P - P%c)/c + 2 = P//c + 2
# For Lean we can write: y = n * (n + 3) / 4 / 3 + 2 = n*(n+3)/12 + 2
# But n*(n+3) ≡ 4 mod 12 (n≡1 mod 4, n+3≡0 mod 4, so n(n+3) ≡ 4 mod 16 at least)
# Wait: n≡1 mod 8, n+3≡4 mod 8. n(n+3)//4 = n*(n+3)/4. Then //3 = n*(n+3)/12.
# For r=337: n=337, n(n+3)/12 = 337*340/12 = 114580/12 = 9548.33... so floor()=9548.
# y = 9548+2 = 9550 ✓

# So y = n*(n+3)/4 // 3 + 2 (integer division thrice, first by 4, then by 3)
# But 4|(n+3) (n≡1 mod 4 => n+3≡0 mod 4), so n*(n+3)/4 is an integer.
# And then P//3 + 2 works!

# For Lean this is: let x := (n+3)/4, let P := n*x = n*(n+3)/4, let y := P/3+2, let z := P*y/(3*y-P)
# This avoids the need for n*(n+3)/12 since the individual divisions are exact.

# For r=337 with c=3: P=n*(n+3)/4

# 3*y - P = 3*9550 - 28645 = 28650 - 28645 = 5

# y = P//c + 2 = floor(n*(n+c)/4 / c) + 2
# c*y - P = c*(P//c + 2) - P = c*2 - (P%c) = 2c - (P%c)

**For r ∈ {337, 457, 697} (c=3):**
- `x = (n+3)/4`
- `P = n*x = n*(n+3)/4`
- `y = P/3 + 2 = n*(n+3)/4/3 + 2`

**For r ∈ {193, 673, 793} (c=7):**

- `P = n*x = n*(n+7)/4`
- `y = P/7 + 2`
- `z = P*y / (7*y - P)` where `7*y - P` is small

Now let me derive z in terms of n:

For `c=3`: `3y = P + 5`, so `z = P*y/5`. Since `y = (P+5)/3` (as `P≡1 mod 3` always), `z = P*(P+5)/(3*5) = P*(P+5)/15`. But `P = n*(n+3)/4`, so `z = n*(n+3)/4 * (n*(n+3)/4 + 5) / 15`. This needs to be an integer.

Actually for Lean, the cleanest formulation is to express everything in terms of `n` directly. Let me compute the formulas:

For r=337 (c=3): x=(n+3)/4, y=n*(n+3)/4/3+2, z=n*(n+3)/4*y/5 where y=n*(n+3)/4/3+2. This is a single expression. In Lean, I can just provide the numeric values `(n+3)/4`, `n*(n+3)/4/3+2`, `n*(n+3)/4*(n*(n+3)/4/3+2)/5` as exact integer expressions and let omega/ring prove everything. Actually, attempting to prove the 337 lemma inline with `nlinarith` and `push_cast` with nat division is going to be very hard. The cleanest approach for these hard residues is to just write them as rational arithmetic lemmas with explicit numeric expressions and `field_simp; ring`. Let me reconsider the strategy.

For Lean, the hard residue lemmas should express witnesses directly in rationals. The parametric formula works, but proving it in Lean requires showing divisibility conditions hold. A much simpler approach: write these as theorems for the existential form only, and use `norm_num` can't handle the parametric case, but `field_simp; ring` can if we avoid integer division.

Let me reformulate: For r=337, n=840k+337, the witnesses are:
- x = (n+3)/4 = (840k+340)/4 = 210k+85
- y = n*(n+3)/4/3 + 2 = n*x/3 + 2 = (210k+85)*(840k+337)/3 + 2

But integer division in Lean 4 is natural number division (floor), which doesn't interact cleanly with `ring`. The trick: since we've proven `4|n+3` and `3|n*x` and `5|n*x`, we can use substitution lemmas.

Actually, let me try a completely different approach: express y and z as explicit polynomial functions of k (not n), then `field_simp; ring` should work. 

For r=337:
- n = 840*k + 337
- x = 210*k + 85
- P = n*x = (840k+337)(210k+85) = 840k*210k + 840k*85 + 337*210k + 337*85 = 176400k²+71400k+70770k+28645 = 176400k²+142170k+28645
- y = P/3 + 2 = (176400k²+142170k+28645)/3 + 2

For y to be a polynomial in k, we need 3|(176400k²+142170k+28645). 
176400=3*58800, 142170=3*47390, 28645=? 28645/3=9548.33... not divisible! 
So P/3 is not polynomial in k. 

But y = P/3 + 2 with integer division is floor(P/3) + 2 = (P - P%3)/3 + 2 = (P-1)/3 + 2 (since P≡1 mod 3).
= (P-1)/3 + 2 = (P+5)/3 = (176400k²+142170k+28650)/3 = 58800k²+47390k+9550.

So y = 58800k² + 47390k + 9550! That's a polynomial in k!

And z = P*y/5:
P*y = n*x*y = (840k+337)(210k+85)(58800k²+47390k+9550)
This is a degree-4 polynomial in k. And 5|P*y since 5|P, so z = P*y/5 is also a polynomial.

This is the key! Let me compute these polynomial forms.

from fractions import Fraction
import math

# Compute polynomial coefficients for x, y, z in terms of k for each hard residue

def poly_witnesses(r, c):
    """For residue r with shift c: x=(n+c)/4, y=(P+5)/3 or (P+10)/7, z=P*y/D"""
    # n = 840*k + r
    # x = (n+c)/4 = (840k+r+c)/4 = 210k + (r+c)/4
    assert (r + c) % 4 == 0, f"r+c={r+c} not div 4"
    x0 = (r+c)//4  # x = 210k + x0
    
    # P = n*x = (840k+r)(210k+x0) = 210*840*k² + (840*x0 + r*210)*k + r*x0
    A_P = 210*840  # 176400
    B_P = 840*x0 + r*210
    C_P = r*x0
    
    # Verify P divisibility by c: P%c must be constant
    for k in range(5):
        P = (840*k+r) * (210*k+x0)
        assert P % c == (r*x0) % c, f"P mod {c} not constant at k={k}: {P%c} vs {(r*x0)%c}"
    
    P_mod_c = (r*x0) % c  # constant
    two_c_minus = 2*c - P_mod_c  # = c*y - P at y = P//c + 2
    
    # y = (P + (c*2 - P_mod_c)) / c -- note: c*y = P + two_c_minus, so y = (P+two_c_minus)/c
    # y = (A_P*k² + B_P*k + C_P + two_c_minus) / c
    y_const_check = (C_P + two_c_minus) % c
    y_lin_check = B_P % c
    y_quad_check = A_P % c
    assert y_const_check == 0 and y_lin_check == 0 and y_quad_check == 0, \
        f"y not polynomial: {y_quad_check}k² + {y_lin_check}k + {y_const_check}"
    
    A_y = A_P // c
    B_y = B_P // c
    C_y = (C_P + two_c_minus) // c
    
    print(f"r={r}, c={c}: x = 210k + {x0}")
    print(f"  P = {A_P}k² + {B_P}k + {C_P}")
    print(f"  P%{c} = {P_mod_c} (constant), 2c-P%c = {two_c_minus}")
    print(f"  y = {A_y}k² + {B_y}k + {C_y}")
    
    # z = P*y/two_c_minus
    # P*y is degree 4 polynomial, divided by two_c_minus
    # Check for k=0,1,2:
    for k in range(4):
        n = 840*k + r
        x_k = 210*k + x0
        P_k = n * x_k
        y_k = A_y*k**2 + B_y*k + C_y
        assert P_k % c == P_mod_c, f"P%c wrong at k={k}"
        ck_y_k = c*y_k
        P_plus = P_k + two_c_minus
        assert ck_y_k == P_plus, f"cy ≠ P+d at k={k}: {ck_y_k} vs {P_plus}"
        # z = P*y / two_c_minus
        z_num = P_k * y_k
        assert z_num % two_c_minus == 0, f"z not integer at k={k}: P*y={z_num}, denom={two_c_minus}"
        z_k = z_num // two_c_minus
        ok = Fraction(4,n) == Fraction(1,x_k) + Fraction(1,y_k) + Fraction(1,z_k)
        print(f"  k={k}: x={x_k}, y={y_k}, z={z_k}, ok={ok}")
    print()

# r=337, c=3
poly_witnesses(337, 3)
# r=457, c=3  
poly_witnesses(457, 3)
# r=697, c=3
poly_witnesses(697, 3)
# r=193, c=7
poly_witnesses(193, 7)
# r=673, c=7
poly_witnesses(673, 7)
# r=793, c=7
poly_witnesses(793, 7)

# The key insight: for r=337, k arbitrary:
# n = 840k+337, x = (n+3)/4 = (840k+340)/4 = 210k+85  (exact int div!)

#   2. For r=337: provide x=210k+85, prove 4*(210k+85) = (840k+337)+3 by omega
#   3. Prove 3*y = n*x+5 where y = n*x/3+2 using Nat.div_eq_of_eq_mul etc.

# For r=673: let's check if period 2 works and find formulas for even/odd k

#   x = (n+7)/4 = (1680j+680)/4 = 420j+170

for r in [673, 793]:
    print(f'r={r} period analysis:')
    for step in range(4):  # try periods 2, 4, 6, 8
        period = 2*(step+1)
        # check if all k within period have same formula
        print(f'  Period {period}:')
        for base in range(period):
            n = 840*base + r
            # Find formula
            found = False
            for c in [3, 5, 7]:
                for shift in [c]:  # x = (n+shift)/4
                    if (n + shift) % 4 != 0:
                        continue
                    x = (n + shift) // 4
                    P = n * x
                    P_mod_c = P % c
                    for add in range(1, 10):
                        y = P // c + add
                        denom = c * y - P
                        if denom > 0 and (P * y) % denom == 0:
                            z = P * y // denom
                            if Fraction(4,n) == Fraction(1,x) + Fraction(1,y) + Fraction(1,z):
                                print(f'    base k={base}: c={c}, add={add}, 7y-P={denom} (i.e. P+{denom}=c*y)')
                                # Check this works for k=base+period as well
                                n2 = 840*(base+period) + r
                                x2 = (n2 + shift) // 4
                                P2 = n2 * x2
                                y2 = P2 // c + add
                                denom2 = c * y2 - P2
                                z2_num = P2 * y2
                                ok = denom2 > 0 and z2_num % denom2 == 0

                                print(f'      k=base+{period}: same formula works: {ok2}')
                                found = True
                                break
                    if found:
                        break
    print()

# Verify formulas for all 6 hard residues
# c3 family: 4x=n+3, 3y=nx+5, 5z=nxy
def c3_check(r, x0):
    for k in range(5):
        n = 840*k + r
        x = 210*k + x0
        assert 4*x == n+3, f"r={r},k={k}: 4x≠n+3"
        P = n*x
        assert P%3 == 1, f"r={r},k={k}: P%3={P%3}≠1"
        y = P//3 + 2
        assert 3*y == P+5, f"r={r},k={k}: 3y≠P+5"
        assert P%5 == 0, f"r={r},k={k}: P%5={P%5}≠0"
        z, rem = divmod(P*y, 5)
        assert rem == 0, f"r={r},k={k}: 5∤P*y"
        # verify 4/n = 1/x + 1/y + 1/z using fractions
        from fractions import Fraction
        lhs = Fraction(4, n)
        rhs = Fraction(1,x) + Fraction(1,y) + Fraction(1,z)
        assert lhs == rhs, f"r={r},k={k}: {lhs}≠{rhs}"
    print(f"r={r} (x0={x0}): c3 family ✓")

c3_check(337, 85)
c3_check(457, 115)
c3_check(697, 175)

# c7-10 family: 4x=n+7, 7y=nx+10, 10z=nxy
def c7_10_check(r, x0):
    for k in range(5):
        n = 840*k + r
        x = 210*k + x0
        assert 4*x == n+7, f"r={r},k={k}: 4x≠n+7"
        P = n*x
        assert P%7 == 4, f"r={r},k={k}: P%7={P%7}≠4"
        y = P//7 + 2
        assert 7*y == P+10, f"r={r},k={k}: 7y≠P+10"
        assert P%10 == 0, f"r={r},k={k}: P%10={P%10}≠0"
        z, rem = divmod(P*y, 10)
        assert rem == 0
        from fractions import Fraction
        assert Fraction(4,n) == Fraction(1,x)+Fraction(1,y)+Fraction(1,z), f"r={r},k={k}: mismatch"
    print(f"r={r} (x0={x0}): c7-10 family ✓")

c7_10_check(193, 50)

# c7-5 family: 4x=n+7, 7y=nx+5, 5z=nxy
def c7_5_check(r, x0, y_add):
    for k in range(5):
        n = 840*k + r
        x = 210*k + x0
        assert 4*x == n+7, f"r={r},k={k}: 4x≠n+7"
        P = n*x
        print(f"  r={r},k={k}: P%7={P%7}, P%5={P%5}")
        y = P//7 + y_add
        assert 7*y == P + 7*y_add - (P%7), f"checking..."
        actual_7y_minus_P = 7*y - P
        print(f"    y_add={y_add}, 7y-P={actual_7y_minus_P}")
        expected = 7*y_add - P%7
        assert actual_7y_minus_P == expected
        if P%5 == 0:
            z, rem = divmod(P*y, 5)
            if rem == 0:
                from fractions import Fraction
                chk = Fraction(4,n) == Fraction(1,x)+Fraction(1,y)+Fraction(1,z)
                print(f"    5z=P*y: ✓, identity: {chk}")

print("r=673 check:")

Let me try an alternative: can we prove `20 ∣ P * y` by showing `20 ∣ P * (P + 20)` (since y = (P+20)/7 and 7 is coprime to 20)? 

Actually y = (P+20)/7. So P*y = P*(P+20)/7. For 20|P*(P+20)/7, since gcd(7,20)=1, we need 20|P*(P+20). And P*(P+20) = P²+20P. 20|P*(P+20) iff 20|P² (since 20|20P). 4|P² iff 2|P (since x even → 2|P), and 5|P² iff 5|P. So 20|P*(P+20) iff 2|P and 5|P iff 10|P. We have 5|P (from x%5=0) and 2|P (from x being even). So 10|P, so 20|P*(P+20), so 20|P*(P+20)/7 = P*y. ✓

This is provable in Lean! `20 ∣ P * y` because:
1. `5 ∣ P` (since `x % 5 = 0`, by `Nat.mul_mod`)
2. `2 ∣ P` (since `x % 2 = 0`, by `Nat.mul_mod`)
3. `10 ∣ P` (combine 1 and 2 via `Nat.Coprime.mul_dvd_of_dvd_of_dvd`)
4. `P * y = P * (P + 20) / 7` and `20 ∣ P * (P + 20)` (since 10|P → 20|P*(P+20))

This is doable but complex. Let me think of an even simpler approach.

from fractions import Fraction

# For r=793, period=2 approach:
# k even: P%4=0, P%20=0, 20|P, so 20|P*y trivially → omega can handle
# k odd:  P%4=2, P%20=10, not 20|P. But 20|P*y still holds.

# Let's try a different x for even k and odd k, but same c=7 structure.
# Alternative: use a period-1 formula with a different helper.

# c7_20 family: 4x=n+7, 7y=nx+20, 20z=nxy. 
# Identity: 4/n = 1/x + 1/y + 1/z
# Let's compute what we need to prove this identity.
# 4/n = (4xyz) / (nxyz) and 1/x+1/y+1/z = (yz+xz+xy)/(xyz)
# So need 4xyz = n(yz+xz+xy).
# From 4x=n+7: n=4x-7. Substitute:
# 4xyz = (4x-7)(yz+xz+xy) = 4xyz - 7yz + 4x²z - 7xz + 4x²y - 7xy
# 0 = -7yz + 4x²z - 7xz + 4x²y - 7xy
# 7yz + 7xz + 7xy = 4x²y + 4x²z = 4x²(y+z)
